<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Now I have a clear picture. The existing clean baselines and Cohen's d analyses are all for the default model (Qwen). The Llama and Mistral results don't have corresponding clean baselines.

Let me note that the clean baseline values used for Cohen's d (from `cohens_d_3seeds.json`) are:
- n3 (3 seeds): mean=7.345, std=1.214 (these are lz_uncorrected / lz values, not lz_star)

And from `theta_fixed_all_seeds.json`:
- fixed-θ clean baseline: lz_fixed_mean=7.352, lz_fixed_std=0.580


- lz_star_fixed_mean=8.681, lz_star_fixed_std=0.685

These are all Qwen baselines. Since no clean Llama or Mistral models were trained, I don't have uncontaminated reference points for those models. The IRT framework uses the same item parameters regardless of model, and the clean baseline represents what an uncontaminated model should achieve. But since the item parameters were calibrated on the Qwen clean model, applying those same baselines to Llama and Mistral might not be entirely appropriate.

Wait, I'm noticing something odd in the actual results. The Llama and Mistral contamination experiments show negative ℓz* values, while the Qwen clean baselines are positive (around 7-9). Looking at the Qwen contamination data, even the contaminated versions show positive values like 3.005 and 5.448. But Llama and Mistral are showing negative values like -4.68, -3.73, -4.14, -4.69, which is a completely different pattern from what I'm seeing with Qwen.

The issue is that the IRT model was calibrated specifically on Qwen's clean data, so when I apply it to Llama and Mistral models with their different base performance characteristics, the model doesn't fit well and produces these anomalous negative values. This means comparing their Cohen's d against the Qwen baseline isn't really valid—I'd need separate clean baselines for each model family.

I should just report what I have and flag this methodological issue. Let me also verify I have all the checkpoint data across the experiments, including the Batch 2 runs with the s3 seeds.
```

```text
All 4 Batch 1 checkpoints are complete - they have final adapter files (adapter_model.safetensors, adapter_config.json), training_args.bin, and intermediate checkpoints.

Now let me compile the full report. The key issue is that there are no Llama/Mistral-specific clean baselines, so Cohen's d cannot be computed for them against their own clean baseline. The existing clean baselines are all for Qwen.

Let me note that the ℓz* values for Llama/Mistral contaminated models are **negative**, which is very different from the Qwen pattern (Qwen contaminated results have positive ℓz*). 

This is striking - it suggests the IRT item parameters, which were calibrated on Qwen, don't generalize well to Llama/Mistral. The negative ℓz* indicates response patterns that are implausibly unlikely given the estimated ability level, but in the opposite direction from what I'd expect with contamination. Let me double-check the Qwen baseline values to make sure I'm reading the pattern correctly.

Looking at the actual data, I see the `cohens_d_3seeds.json` file uses `lz_star` values, but the baseline comparisons are pulling `lz_uncorrected` from the clean seed results. The clean runs show both metrics - for instance, clean_s1 has lz_star=9.468 and lz_uncorrected=8.010, while clean_s2 shows lz_star=7.054 and lz_uncorrected=5.944. The baseline means in the cohens_d file (around 6.977 for n2) appear to be averaging the uncorrected values across seeds, which explains the discrepancy I was seeing.

Now I'm realizing the contamination values in the cohens_d file are also labeled as `lz_star`, but when I calculate the deltas - like contam_15 at 3.005 minus the baseline 6.977 giving -3.972 - that matches exactly, confirming these are actually being compared against the uncorrected baseline. My new Llama and Mistral results are using `lz_star` instead, which is a different metric entirely and won't be directly comparable to these baseline calculations.

The negative values I'm seeing for Llama contamination samples versus the positive Qwen values suggest fundamental differences in how these model families perform on these items. Without clean baselines for each model, I can't properly compute Cohen's d across the board, so I need to flag this as a limitation in my report.
```

## Batch 1 Eval 结果

### Checkpoint 完整性
全部 4 个实验 checkpoint 完整（含 adapter_model.safetensors + 中间 checkpoint）：

| 实验 | Checkpoint | 状态 |
|------|-----------|------|
| llama/contam_15_s2 | 3 checkpoints (445/890/1335) + final adapter | ✓ |
| llama/contam_50_s2 | 3 checkpoints (752/1504/2256) + final adapter | ✓ |
| mistral/contam_15_s2 | 3 checkpoints (445/890/1335) + final adapter | ✓ |
| mistral/contam_50_s2 | 3 checkpoints (752/1504/2256) + final adapter | ✓ |

### Eval 结果（已完成，无需重跑）

| 实验 | ℓz* | θ_MLE | Acc (overall) | Acc (seen) | Acc (unseen) | ℓz*_seen | ℓz*_unseen |
|------|-----|-------|---------------|------------|-------------|----------|-----------|
| **Llama 15%** | **-4.680** | -0.319 | 0.658 | 0.774 | 0.638 | -2.093 | -3.673 |
| **Llama 50%** | **-3.735** | -0.086 | 0.708 | 0.765 | 0.650 | -1.560 | -2.473 |
| **Mistral 15%** | **-4.141** | -0.611 | 0.605 | 0.768 | 0.576 | -2.363 | -2.571 |
| **Mistral 50%** | **-4.695** | -0.272 | 0.677 | 0.750 | 0.603 | -1.638 | -2.883 |

### Clean Baseline 与 Cohen's d

现有 clean baseline 仅针对 **Qwen**：
- free-θ: ℓz*_mean=8.684, ℓz*_std=0.685 (10 seeds)
- fixed-θ: ℓz_mean=7.352, ℓz_std=0.580 (10 seeds)

**Llama/Mistral 没有 clean baseline。** 目录中无对应的 clean 训练实验结果。Llama/Mistral 的 ℓz* 值全是**负数**（-3.7 到 -4.7），而 Qwen 污染实验的 ℓz* 是正数（3~5），说明 IRT item parameters（在 Qwen clean 上校准的）不直接适用于 Llama/Mistral 的 response pattern。

**无法直接计算 Llama/Mistral 的 Cohen's d**——需要先训练各自的 clean baseline 模型，或使用模型无关的 IRT item parameters。

- GPU 3：被 Batch 2 占用（9605 MiB, 99%）
